=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array nums consisting of n prime integers.
You need to construct an array ans of length n, such that, for each index i, the bitwise OR of ans[i] and ans[i] + 1 is equal to nums[i], i.e. ans[i] OR (ans[i] + 1) == nums[i].
Additionally, you must minimize each value of ans[i] in the resulting array.
If it is not possible to find such a value for ans[i] that satisfies the condition, then set ans[i] = -1.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,3,5,7]
Output: [-1,1,4,3]
Explanation:

For i = 0, as there is no value for ans[0] that satisfies ans[0] OR (ans[0] + 1) = 2, so ans[0] = -1.
For i = 1, the smallest ans[1] that satisfies ans[1] OR (ans[1] + 1) = 3 is 1, because 1 OR (1 + 1) = 3.
For i = 2, the smallest ans[2] that satisfies ans[2] OR (ans[2] + 1) = 5 is 4, because 4 OR (4 + 1) = 5.
For i = 3, the smallest ans[3] that satisfies ans[3] OR (ans[3] + 1) = 7 is 3, because 3 OR (3 + 1) = 7.


Example 2:

Input: nums = [11,13,31]
Output: [9,12,15]
Explanation:

For i = 0, the smallest ans[0] that satisfies ans[0] OR (ans[0] + 1) = 11 is 9, because 9 OR (9 + 1) = 11.
For i = 1, the smallest ans[1] that satisfies ans[1] OR (ans[1] + 1) = 13 is 12, because 12 OR (12 + 1) = 13.
For i = 2, the smallest ans[2] that satisfies ans[2] OR (ans[2] + 1) = 31 is 15, because 15 OR (15 + 1) = 31.


 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 100
2 <= nums[i] <= 1000
nums[i] is a prime number.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minBitwiseArray(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minBitwiseArray(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = []
        for num in nums:
            if num % 2 == 0:
                ans.append(-1)
                continue
            r = 0
            while (num >> r) & 1:
                r += 1
            ans.append(num - (1 << (r - 1)))
        return ans

Let's maybe include type hints. Code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minBitwiseArray(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = []
        for num in nums:
            if num % 2 == 0:
                ans.append(-1)
                continue
            
            r = 0
            while (num >> r) & 1:
                r += 1
            
            ans.append(num - (1 << (r - 1)))
        
        return ans
```

Given x, let t = number of trailing 1s of x. Then x's binary: `...?` Let high bits above t. Since bit t=0 and lower t bits=1. x+1: lower t bits 0, bit t=1, high bits same. OR: lower t bits 1, bit t=1, high bits same. So n = (high bits) followed by bit t=1 and lower t bits=1. This means n's trailing ones count r is at least t+1. Also x = n - 2^t? Let's verify with high bits: n = high * 2^{t+1} + (2^{t+1}-1). x = high * 2^{t+1} + (2^t -1). Difference = 2^t. Yes. So for any valid x, t <= r-1. Since x = n - 2^t, to minimize x, maximize t. Now, final code. We'll output:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minBitwiseArray(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        ans = []
        for num in nums:
            if num % 2 == 0:
                ans.append(-1)
                continue

            r = 0
            while (num >> r) & 1:
                r += 1

            ans.append(num - (1 << (r - 1)))

        return ans
```
